Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm trying to lose weight, exactly. It's more about just... staying streamlined, I guess? I hope that doesn't sound bad, I just want to be in the best shape for the physical demands of the job. 

So, I suppose I do eat a bit less than some of the other guys. Most days I just keep it to black coffee... maybe a boiled egg or a little tuna. It's really all I need to keep going, and it doesn't slow me down. And, um, if I'm not moving freight on a shift—like if I have a day off or something—I just tell myself I don't need a real meal anyway, since I'm not burning off the energy. 

It just makes logical sense to me, to only fuel up when you're doing the heavy lifting. I hope that's an okay way to look at it? I'm definitely not trying to do anything unhealthy, I promise.

I hope that answers your question.\n\nDoctor: Have you been intentionally trying to lose weight or keep your weight low, even if it meant eating less or exercising more than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm trying to lose weight, exactly. It's more about just... staying streamlined, I guess? I hope that doesn't sound bad, I just want to be in the best shape for the physical demands of the job. \n\nSo, I suppose I do eat a bit less than some of the other guys. Most days I just keep it to black coffee... maybe a boiled egg or a little tuna. It's really all I need to keep going, and it doesn't slow me down. And, um, if I'm not moving freight on a shift—like if I have a day off or something—I just tell myself I don't need a real meal anyway, since I'm not burning off the energy. \n\nIt just makes logical sense to me, to only fuel up when you're doing the heavy lifting. I start to feel like... well, like if I'm not tight-looking, then I'm just not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. I just want to prove I still have that same drive and work ethic, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too crazy. Is it normal to want to hold onto that kind of discipline? Well, um... I don't know if I'd use the word "fear," exactly. That makes it sound a little extreme, doesn't it? I just like to be careful. I really hope you don't think I'm being unreasonable or anything, but... well, if I were to eat something heavy, like rice or bread, or maybe something a bit greasy, I just... I can’t stop thinking that it’s going to sit on me right away. Like it'll just instantly stick to my waist and slow me down on the floor. 

So, um, if I do happen to eat something like that, I usually make sure to check the scale the next morning, or I'll pull on my work belt to see how it fits. It's really just to make sure I didn’t let myself go, you know? I just want to stay on top of things before they get out of hand. I think it's just about being proactive and staying disciplined. That's a normal way to handle it, right? I just want to be sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to do.

Well, um... I guess I don't really know what other people see when they look at me. I hope they don't think I look bad or anything. 

But when I look in the mirror... I just see someone who still has a bit of work to do. Like, I can still see some softness around the middle, you know? Just places where I could be a bit tighter so I don't get sloppy on the job. I don't think I look huge or anything like that, I just... I see the spots where I need to stay disciplined. 

Is that what you mean? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer. I really try to be honest with myself about where I need to improve, I hope that's okay.

Well, um... I really wouldn't say it causes me distress, exactly. I don't want to make it sound like I'm falling apart over it or anything.

Well, um... I really wouldn't say it interferes with my life too much. I mean, my relationships are fine, and I still do the things I need to do. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around obsessing all day.

I can’t stop thinking that it’s going to sit on me right away. I just want to be sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: When you look at yourself in the mirror, do you see yourself as larger or less lean than others might perceive you to be?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Well, um... I wouldn't really say I get upset or anxious about it. I mean, I don't want you to think I'm losing sleep over how I look or anything like that. I'm a grown man, you know? 

I guess sometimes I just want to make sure I'm keeping myself in check. But I wouldn't call it anxiety. I just like to stay on top of things, so I don't let myself go. Other people might say I look fine, but they don't know what it takes to keep up with the physical demands of my work. 

I'm just trying to maintain my standards, that's all. I really hope that doesn't sound like I'm overly worried or anything. I'm just being practical. Does that sound like a normal way to look at it?

Well, um... I really don't want to sound vain or anything. I mean, I'm 55, I know I'm not a kid anymore. I'm not standing in front of the mirror crying or anything dramatic like that, I promise.

And, um, if I'm not moving freight on a shift—like if I have a day off or something—I just tell myself I don't need a real meal anyway, since I'm not burning off the energy. \n\nIt just makes logical sense to me, to only fuel up when you're doing the heavy lifting. I'm definitely not trying to do anything unhealthy, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your job performance or self-image depends on you staying very lean, even if others might see it as too thin?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know if \"depends\" is exactly the right word, but I suppose there's some truth to that. I really hope it doesn't sound vain or anything. It's just... well, at my age, you really have to work hard to keep your edge, right? \n\nSo, um, when I look in the mirror... especially, you know, at my stomach and my waist... if I don't see myself looking smaller and tight, it really bothers me. I start to feel like... well, like if I'm not tight-looking, then I'm just not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. I just want to prove I still have that same drive and work ethic, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too crazy. Is it normal to want to hold onto that kind of discipline? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel intense fear about gaining weight or find yourself going to great lengths to prevent weight gain, even if it means restricting food or pushing yourself harder physically?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't know if I'd use the word \"fear,\" exactly. That makes it sound a little extreme, doesn't it? I just like to be careful. I really hope you don't think I'm being unreasonable or anything, but... well, if I were to eat something heavy, like rice or bread, or maybe something a bit greasy, I just... I can’t stop thinking that it’s going to sit on me right away. Like it'll just instantly stick to my waist and slow me down on the floor. \n\nSo, um, if I do happen to eat something like that, I usually make sure to check the scale the next morning, or I'll pull on my work belt to see how it fits. It's really just to make sure I didn’t let myself go, you know? I just want to stay on top of things before they get out of hand. I think it's just about being proactive and staying disciplined. That's a normal way to handle it, right? I just want to be sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: When you look at yourself in the mirror, do you see yourself as larger or less lean than others might perceive you to be?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I don't really know what other people see when they look at me. I hope they don't think I look bad or anything. \n\nBut when I look in the mirror... I just see someone who still has a bit of work to do. Like, I can still see some softness around the middle, you know? Just places where I could be a bit tighter so I don't get sloppy on the job. I don't think I look huge or anything like that, I just... I see the spots where I need to stay disciplined. \n\nIs that what you mean? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer. I really try to be honest with myself about where I need to improve, I hope that's okay.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how you see yourself when you look in the mirror, compared to how others might see you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm sorry, I think that's sort of what I was just trying to explain a second ago. I hope I didn't confuse things or explain it poorly. \n\nI honestly don't really know how other people see me. The guys at the warehouse don't really talk about that kind of stuff, so I'm not entirely sure. I'd like to think they just see a guy who still pulls his weight and works hard, you know? \n\nBut as for me, when I look in the mirror... it's really just like I was saying. I don't see myself as completely out of shape or anything extreme like that. I just see the spots where I'm getting a little soft, the parts where I need to be stricter so I don't lose my edge on the floor. \n\nI hope that makes sense. Did I answer that right, or were you asking about something else? I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, I really just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that even if you lost more weight, you still wouldn't be satisfied with how you look?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think it's like that. I really hope I'm not giving you the impression that I'm taking things too far or being unreasonable. \n\nI think if I just... tightened up those few spots I was talking about, I'd be completely satisfied. I'm not trying to just keep going forever or anything. I just want to get to a good, solid place where I know I can handle the heavy lifting at work without feeling sluggish. Once I get to that point, I'll be happy with it. \n\nThat makes sense, right? I'm really just aiming for a healthy standard so I can do my job well, not trying to overdo it. I hope you can see I'm just trying to be responsible about it.\n\nDoctor: Even though your weight is low, do you find yourself frequently thinking about your body shape or weight in a way that causes you distress or influences what you eat or how you exercise?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really wouldn't say it causes me distress, exactly. I don't want to make it sound like I'm falling apart over it or anything. \n\nIt's more just... well, when I look in the mirror, especially at my stomach and my waist... if I'm not smaller and, you know, tight-looking in those areas, I start to feel like I'm losing my grip. Like I'm not that same disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. \n\nSo I guess it does influence what I eat, just because I want to keep that discipline up and prove I can still handle the work. I just want to stay sharp. Is it okay to think like that? I'm just trying to be honest with you, I really hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit, use laxatives, or push yourself to exercise excessively after eating to control your weight or shape?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, I don't do anything like that. I really hope you don't think I'm doing things like making myself sick or taking pills. I'd never do that, I promise. \n\nAs for the exercise... well, I do work out, sure. But it's just to stay in shape for the warehouse, like I said. Sometimes if I feel like I've eaten a bit too much, maybe I'll push myself a little harder on a run or do some extra reps, but it's not excessive. It's just... normal discipline, you know? Just making sure I'm earning my keep. \n\nI'm not doing anything dangerous or crazy, I swear. I hope you believe me. Does that sound reasonable to you?\n\nDoctor: Do your concerns about your body shape or weight interfere with your daily life, such as affecting your work, relationships, or how you participate in activities?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really wouldn't say it interferes with my life too much. I mean, my relationships are fine, and I still do the things I need to do. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around obsessing all day. \n\nBut, well... there is this one thing. I was supposed to start a new logistics job recently. It's a great opportunity, really. But... they won't clear me to start until I get this, um, this whole weight thing addressed. \n\nIt's a little frustrating, to be honest, because I keep telling them—and myself—that I'm ready to work. I feel fine! But they're making me put my plans on hold for now. I guess they just want to be extra careful, which is nice of them, but I'm really hoping to just get this sorted so I can get back to work. \n\nI'm doing everything they asked by coming here, so I hope that shows I'm being cooperative. I'm really trying my best to handle it all properly. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt upset or anxious specifically about your body shape or weight, even when other people might not see a problem?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't really say I get upset or anxious about it. I mean, I don't want you to think I'm losing sleep over how I look or anything like that. I'm a grown man, you know? \n\nI guess sometimes I just want to make sure I'm keeping myself in check. But I wouldn't call it anxiety. I just like to stay on top of things, so I don't let myself go. Other people might say I look fine, but they don't know what it takes to keep up with the physical demands of my work. \n\nI'm just trying to maintain my standards, that's all. I really hope that doesn't sound like I'm overly worried or anything. I'm just being practical. Does that sound like a normal way to look at it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt distressed or upset specifically about certain parts of your body, like your stomach or waist, even when others might not see a problem with how you look?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really don't want to sound vain or anything. I mean, I'm 55, I know I'm not a kid anymore. I'm not standing in front of the mirror crying or anything dramatic like that, I promise. \n\nBut, well... I guess when I do look in the mirror, sometimes... especially at my stomach and my waist... it just bothers me a little. If it's not, you know, smaller and tight-looking, I just feel like... like I'm losing my edge. Like I'm not the disciplined, hard-working man I used to be on the warehouse floor. \n\nI just want to look like someone who can still pull his weight, you know? It's more about pride in my work ethic than just looks. I hope you can understand where I'm coming from with that. It's not too unusual for a guy my age to want to stay sharp, right?\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about staying lean ever caused you to avoid situations, like social events or time with family, because you were worried about how you looked or what food might be there?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, not really. I mean, I definitely don't want you to think I'm, like, hiding away from the world or anything like that. I'm a pretty regular guy, you know? \n\nI still see my family, and if there's a get-together, I'll go. I don't let this whole thing stop me from living my life. I might just, you know, be a little careful about what I put on my plate if there's a lot of heavy stuff around, but I don't avoid the whole event. I wouldn't want to be rude to the host or make my family think I don't want to see them. \n\nI just try to make smart choices while I'm there. That's just being responsible, isn't it? I hope that shows I'm still keeping a good balance with everything.\n\nDoctor: When you look at yourself in the mirror or think about your body, do you feel that certain parts of you, like your stomach or waist, appear bigger than they actually are?\n\nPatient: Well, um...
